## 8. Wave-21 release evolution

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever (EVOL), 2026-10-21; target 0.4.37.
This amendment supersedes the insert-only input policy in §3. Ordinary
writes while the exact declaration is active refresh existing native input
fingerprints. They do not create native birth evidence for migrated rows.
Active forward receipts refresh only actually edited, currently authored
non-output inputs and their reference lifetime bindings. Overwritten historical
inputs, original output preimages, omission interpretations on unrelated
writes, inactive receipts and immutable definitions retain their custody.
An older-release edit does not receive this refresh. At re-entry a changed
input is compatible when deterministic recomputation over the assembled source
produces the exact stored output; an incompatible edit without accepted native evidence still refuses.
Valid native outputs written under the owning declaration remain authored
values, even when they differ from the historical transform. Refresh replaces
the fingerprint, rather than accumulating old accepted images.

Rename destination conflicts consult only physically present or retained cells.
An absent optional destination is absent; interpreting it as null or a default
cannot manufacture a conflict. Explicit present null is still a real cell.

SEC6 owns rule-changing deploy review items, write intent, projection privacy
and client session validation; EVOL does not change those contracts.


A table declares `table observations renamed from behaviors:`. It moves the
same numeric table identity and row/reference lifetimes, never copies rows or
merges independent histories. Both names cannot appear together in a selected
schema. The durable name relation permits old-source rollback and older-source
writes to return under the newer name. Existing independent destination storage
refuses atomically. Indexes, retained type definitions, asset homes and migration
receipts follow that identity; literal values and operation names do not change.
Store format 8 and the release-table-renames-v1 capability fence the new metadata.

`rollback --check --bridge DIR` now writes the selected target's complete source,
loosening only differing compatible value domains to their union with current
source and retaining new authoritative tables with their current rules. It lists
changes and direct target read/write operations on affected tables. It never changes immutable
source or activates automatically. The generated bridge is a new deployable
release, subject to ordinary compilation/adoption. Incompatible nominal types,
new business logic, current guards absent from v1, and old UI assumptions about
null require explicit review; diagnostics and the app-specific checklist remain
visible. A retained new table does not claim a frozen v1 operation exposes it.
No reverse projection fabricates an asset, reference, list element or missing
table. Scalar `down` remains the answer for a truthful scalar substitution;
loosened bridges cover nullable files/images, empty image lists and optional
money without invented placeholders.

Preview causes are recorded by the actual narrowed-population closure: a column
and incompatible value category, or a typed reference to a hidden parent. Counts
may overlap across causes and are schema populations before read rules. New-table
rows have a separate cause. Foresight consults declared reverse writers rather
than calling their old output absent. SEC6 owns review-item resurfacing.

Asset columns (`file`, image/video and asset lists) use `renamed from` as an
identity move, rather than computed scalar forward/down substitutions. The
active and retained physical cells and the ledger's one home move together;
selecting old source changes the public name back, preserving current values.
Both aliases cannot be active together; a conflicting physical/retained cell
refuses atomically. Reference/captured/engine field renames remain excluded.
Older runtimes refuse store format 8; device projections omit migration metadata.

Cross-table historical existence and unique point reads are first-class
backfill inputs under §2's assembled-source, metered system authority. A guide
example must include the source index and explicit row input rather than an
application reconciliation loop. Outputs remain disjoint scalar patches and
all declarations observe the same pre-patch snapshot.

`fileText(file)` is a migration-only deterministic input, accepted in backfill
and down expressions, including through pure helpers. It reads the immutable
retained file named by the ledger, as UTF-8. It never reads an application path
or URL, unverified bytes, arbitrary ids, or a device's filesystem. The runtime
requires an internal migration context and charges the job heap and step meter
before reading at most the ledger byte length plus one. Absent bytes, released
assets, length disagreement, invalid UTF-8 or meter exhaustion refuse the whole
adoption. Preview reads the same original immutable asset root while writing
only its private database snapshot. The feature creates no user-callable
migration operation and does not make arbitrary file contents readable to users.
`nonemptyLines(text)` counts trimmed nonempty lines, including repeated entries
separately; CRLF and whitespace-only lines have their ordinary text meaning.
It is a pure scalar transform; an empty textual list counts zero. A generated
scalar is reversible through the existing output-preimage/evidence custody;
fileText never mutates the bytes or their home. Ordinary new-order byte validation
is outside this release-migration primitive; it receives no invented enforcement
claim from a historical backfill.


### EVOL review clarifications

“Exact declaration” means its normalized typed-IR digest, not a release number.
A declaration can be carried in later source. “Currently authored” means a
column present in that selected schema whose interpreted value actually changes
in an application write; hidden inputs and overwritten migration arguments are
excluded. “Older-release edit” means a write while this exact declaration is
absent. Replacing accepted non-output inputs intentionally forgets their previous
live values: this is live data, not a history of every application write. It
never forgets an overwritten output's reversal preimage. For migrated rows the
parent's dependency/output comparison still applies, with exact recomputation
agreement added for both transformed and fully reversed cells. Native rows with
matching refreshed non-output fingerprints and valid complete outputs preserve
their authored images. A fingerprint mismatch needs full recomputation agreement
or refuses; it never authorizes overwriting a different authored image.

A helper inherits the migration context transitively when expanded. Calling a
helper containing fileText outside a backfill/down refuses during compilation;
raw IR also refuses at execution without the unforgeable internal context/root.
Failure in a forward migration, down, or exact preview aborts that entire
transaction. Preview has the identical job precharge and file validation.
File-read failures never print raw bytes. Output scalars are deliberately
authored data under their destination rules; an author copying file text into
a readable text column must choose its audience explicitly, as for any backfill.
No automatic output or diagnostic exposes the input bytes. Asserted missing
points refuse the whole operation. Nullable points preserve §2's ordinary
nullable semantics only through the author's explicit expression; no missing
point becomes an invented preimage. The database snapshot pins ledger and point
reads; asset bytes are immutable under their ledger id, read at exact length.

Rename conflicts compare a destination cell not owned by the moving alias in
the same surviving row lifetime. They do not compare the source cell against
itself or consult dead row lifetimes. Table moves keep their numeric identity;
independent destination identities cannot be merged. Asset renames never revive
a released asset. A selected schema still validates every moved asset against
the ledger/type. Reference, captured and engine-field renames are diagnosed
before activation, rather than silently losing their bindings.

The bridge's compatible joins are: identical underlying types with optionality
union; integer bounds with the lower minimum and higher maximum; matching-kind assets
with the higher byte limit (file MIME and image-once policy unchanged);
same-format text with the lower minimum and higher maximum; enum member
union; lists with recursively compatible element types, lower minimum and higher
maximum. Other nominal/type conversions are not guessed; they remain target
domains, are listed for explicit typed repair, and receive no promise of complete
visibility. Existing compiler diagnostics and the checklist expose operations
needing adaptation; readers AND writers on affected tables are listed. The
bridge never executes a scalar asset projection: it changes only the selected
schema's value domain over the same retained actual cells. Old nullable UI
assumptions remain an application compatibility check, not a claimed guarantee.
It keeps one alias for a renamed table, selecting the target's spelling; the
other name remains only in the durable identity relation. That spelling routes
old reads/writes to the same numeric row lifetime. It never adds both aliases as
independent tables. Existing target rule/intent text remains byte-identical;
new tables retain current rule text. Ordinary deploy preview reports current
safeguards the target lacks. SEC6 owns rule-review resurfacing; EVOL adds no
blocking review gate or invented safety guarantee.

A cause is either `(table,column,incompatible-value-category,target-type)` or
`(table,column,hidden-parent-table)`. Activation first hides incompatible rows,
then iterates typed reference edges until no additional dependent hides. Only rows physically in the current active `r` namespace immediately before
activation contribute to these preview counts; already dormant rows do not.
Neither source nor target application read rules filter this denominator. A row may have more
than one cause, so cause counts are explicitly non-additive; total hidden rows
are distinct. New-table rows are counted separately. Static foresight compares
possible future domains and declared reverse writers; exact preview runs the
actual activation against current data. Both are privileged local operator
outputs, never per-user query results or public HTTP metadata. Private preview
writes are removed, never published to the live database or generation.


The native and migrated re-entry algorithms are distinct. Native acceptance is
available only when the row has NO forward evidence receipt for the declaration
and has its native marker. A migrated row NEVER gains that marker from an edit.
For each migrated row, every re-entry evaluates the patch again against its
reconstructed inputs and current point dependencies, regardless of whether saved
inputs compare equal. Refreshing its non-output inputs/bindings changes that
input basis, not its `after` computed-result baseline or output preimages. If
fresh computation differs from saved `after`, a stored output equal to the old
computed result, reversed preimage, or fresh computation permits writing the
fresh computation, never retaining a stale value; a different manual output
conflicts. Successful adoption records the fresh computation as `after`. If inputs and computation are unchanged,
the parent's explicit edited-added-cell preservation rule applies. Thus a live
retarget P1 → P2 with O=f(P1) never skips computation at re-entry: it either
recomputes to f(P2) or refuses a conflicting edited O. Backfill is an activation
transform, not steady-state maintained-data enforcement. A native image whose
current input fingerprint matches its accepted native marker instead preserves
its complete valid authored outputs; a mismatching native image needs exact full
recomputation agreement or refuses. These branches cannot substitute for each
other.

Bridge operation listing covers direct lowered Quarry read/write table sites,
including expanded pure helpers. It is a conservative table-level diagnostic,
not a complete dependency proof: indirect rule dependencies, actions, external
workers and frozen UI assumptions require the checklist's manual workflow audit.
The lack of a complete-visibility promise for incompatible type conversions says
nothing about the separate operation-listing diagnostic. No listing grants
authority to deploy or replaces ordinary compiler/adoption validation.

For avoidance of doubt, “active” in visibility accounting is a storage state,
not a viewer's source-rule result. With 1,000 physical active rows and 400 denied
to some viewer, all 1,000 are in the privileged preview denominator. With 600
physical active rows and 400 already dormant, only 600 are in that denominator.
If the target hides 100 of those 600, `hiddenRows` is 100. No source/target rule
predicate is executed to convert that into a per-user number. This preserves the
parent DORM contract and cannot leak to an app caller: the tool is local operator
inspection only.


In the migrated branch, `deps_same` means BOTH input values and fresh computed
patch equal their saved counterparts. If inputs change but the patch equals
saved `after`, `deps_same` is false: the fresh patch is written when the current
output equals saved `after`, its preimage, or that same fresh patch; any other
manual value refuses. Matching more than one allowed baseline has no precedence
ambiguity: each allows writing the same single fresh patch. Only `deps_same`
plus a still-transformed, present output invokes the parent's explicit preservation
of edited added cells. Every successful staged receipt records fresh inputs and
fresh `after`, retaining the correct predecessor preimages.

If both a native marker and a forward receipt for the same digest are present,
the receipt takes precedence: evaluate the migrated algorithm and its exact
baseline/preimage custody. The marker cannot skip that computation. This overlap
is not produced by ordinary writes (which never create a native marker on a
migrated row), but precedence is explicit for retained/legacy state. Pruned
membership is checked first and cannot become native acceptance. Down receipts
have distinct down digests and do not establish native-forward status.

“Affected” for bridge listing includes domain-loosened tables, retained new
authoritative tables, and identities whose table/asset-column spelling moves.
Use the target spelling for a renamed identity, with its current spelling noted
as provenance; never list the aliases as two independent tables. Emit direct
lowered Quarry readers/writers on those tables. The separate manual audit still
covers indirect rule/action/worker/UI dependencies.


### EVOL readiness record
## 9. Edited migrated outputs (ROLL)

Commissioned for 0.4.39, 2026-10-22. LLP 3000.000.000 §20 and its review
clarifications govern default rollback views and the closed safety boundary.
An in-place output edited under its declaring source may use a declared down
on its current assembled image, or keep the exact value in a target domain
which already admits it. A failing old type/constraint still refuses; no
per-row widening or invented scalar is allowed. Unedited outputs keep exact
preimage precedence, with a receipt saying when that old view is stale until
forward. Real forward conflicts include competing values and bridge repair.

Receipts distinguish the original preimage, authored newer image, saved
computation, selected old physical image and presence. Equal explicit inputs
alone cannot authorize retaining an edited output: fresh point/existence
recomputation must also agree with the saved computation, or exactly equal
the actual authored image. Changed projected cells, parents, pruned evidence
and meter cuts still refuse atomically. Editing never creates native-birth
status. Format 9 fences this retained law, including after selecting old source;
format 8 remains readable and upgrades transactionally on successful adoption.
Repeated cycles, partially retired outputs and table-name changes preserve
that evidence. The implementation adds focused regressions for those paths.

     1	//! Typed one-child extraction; home transfer is an activation operation.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — an asset is moved with its one home.
     3	use super::*;
     4	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     5	pub struct Extraction {
     6	    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
     7	    pub each: bool,
     8	    pub child: String,
     9	    pub parent: String,
    10	    pub reference: String,
    11	    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    12	}
    13	impl Extraction {
    14	    pub fn admit(&self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), String> {
    15	        if schema.extractions.iter().filter(|e|e.child==self.child).count()>1 {
    16	            return Err("one extraction declaration owns each child table; native children cannot be assigned to competing sources".into());
    17	        }
    18	        let child = schema
    19	            .table(&self.child)
    20	            .ok_or("extraction child is not declared")?;
    21	        if self.child == self.parent
    22	            || schema.table(&self.parent).is_none()
    23	            || child.ephemeral.is_some()
    24	            || schema.maintain_for(&self.child).is_some()
    25	        {
    26	            return Err("extraction needs distinct authoritative durable parent and child tables without captured ownership".into());
    27	        }
    28	        if child.columns.get(&self.reference)
    29	            != Some(&ColumnType::Ref {
    30	                table: self.parent.clone(),
    31	            })
    32	        {
    33	            return Err("extraction needs a required reference to its parent".into());
    34	        }
    35	        if !matches!(
    36	            child.on_delete.get(&self.reference),
    37	            Some(references::OnDelete::Cascade | references::OnDelete::Restrict)
    38	        ) {
    39	            return Err("reversible extraction requires on delete cascade or restrict; kept references cannot prove the parent lifetime".into());
    40	        }
    41	        if self.fields.is_empty()
    42	            || self.fields.contains_key(&self.reference)
    43	            || self.fields.keys().any(|c| {
    44	                !child.columns.contains_key(c)
    45	                    || c == "id"
    46	                    || child.engine_field(c)
    47	                    || child.maintained.contains_key(c)
    48	                    || child.sequences.contains_key(c)
    49	                    || child.written_by.contains_key(c)
    50	            })
    51	        {
    52	            return Err("extraction fields must be distinct authoritative child columns".into());
    53	        }
    54	        if self.each {
    55	            let assets:Vec<_>=self.fields.keys().filter(|c|asset(&child.columns[*c])).collect();
    56	            if assets.len()!=1 || !matches!(child.columns[assets[0]],ColumnType::Image{..}|ColumnType::File{..}|ColumnType::Video{..}) {
    57	                return Err("extract each requires exactly one required scalar image/file/video destination; map its historical bounded list".into());
    58	            }
    59	        }
    60	        if !self.fields.keys().any(|c| asset(&child.columns[c])) {
    61	            return Err("extraction needs at least one mapped image/file column".into());
    62	        }
    63	        if child.columns.iter().any(|(c, k)| {
    64	            c != &self.reference
    65	                && c != "id"
    66	                && !self.fields.contains_key(c)
    67	                && !k.is_optional()
    68	                && !child.defaults.contains_key(c)
    69	                && !child.engine_field(c)
    70	        }) {
    71	            return Err("map every required child column or give it an explicit default".into());
    72	        }
    73	        Ok(())
    74	    }
    75	}
    76	pub(super) fn asset(k: &ColumnType) -> bool {
    77	    match k {
    78	        ColumnType::Optional(k)
    79	        | ColumnType::List { of: k, .. }
    80	        | ColumnType::ListRange { of: k, .. } => asset(k),
    81	        ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } => true,
    82	        _ => false,
    83	    }
    84	}

    32	fn key(tx:&Tx<'_>,e:&Extraction)->Result<String,RuntimeError>{

    34	    let aliases:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_asset_column_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;

    50	fn load(tx: &Tx<'_>, t: i64, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    51	    let bytes: Option<i64> = tx
    52	        .conn()
    53	        .query_row(
    54	            "SELECT octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",
    55	            params![t, id],
    56	            |r| r.get(0),
    57	        )
    58	        .optional()?;
    59	    let Some(bytes) = bytes else { return Ok(None) };
    60	    Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort).heap(
    61	        (bytes.max(0) as u64)
    62	            .saturating_mul(64)
    63	            .saturating_add(8192),
    64	    )?;
    65	    let text: String = tx.conn().query_row(
    66	        "SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",
    67	        params![t, id],
    68	        |r| r.get(0),
    69	    )?;
    70	    serde_json::from_str(&text)
    71	        .map(Some)
    72	        .map_err(|e| refuse(e.to_string()))
    73	}

    76	    Ok(())
    77	}
    78	fn value(

    98	}
    99	fn transfer(
   100	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
   101	    v: &Value,
   102	    from: (&str, &str, &str),
   103	    to: (&str, &str, &str),
   104	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   105	    if let Value::Array(vs) = v {
   106	        for v in vs {
   107	            transfer(tx, v, from, to)?;
   108	        }
   109	        return Ok(());
   110	    }
   111	    if v == &Value::Null {
   112	        return Ok(());
   113	    }
   114	    let id = crate::assets::asset_id(Some(v))
   115	        .ok_or_else(|| refuse("extraction asset has no typed ledger id"))?;
   116	    let bytes:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT bytes FROM _assets WHERE id=?1 AND home_table=?2 AND home_id=?3 AND home_column=?4 AND released_at IS NULL",params![id,from.0,from.1,from.2],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   117	    let bytes = bytes.ok_or_else(|| {
   118	        refuse(format!(
   119	            "extraction asset {id} has no live source home; never claim or duplicate it"
   120	        ))
   121	    })?;
   122	    let root = tx
   123	        .meta("activation_asset_root")?
   124	        .ok_or_else(|| refuse("extraction requires activation asset custody"))?;
   125	    if id.is_empty()
   126	        || !id
   127	            .bytes()
   128	            .all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'-' || b == b'_')
   129	    {
   130	        return Err(refuse("invalid extraction asset id"));
   131	    }
   132	    let path = std::path::Path::new(&root).join(id);
   133	    let meta = std::fs::symlink_metadata(&path)
   134	        .map_err(|_| refuse(format!("extraction asset {id} bytes are unavailable")))?;
   135	    if !meta.is_file() || meta.len() != bytes as u64 {
   136	        return Err(refuse(format!(
   137	            "extraction asset {id} byte length disagrees with its ledger"
   138	        )));
   139	    }
   140	    tx.conn().execute(
   141	        "UPDATE _assets SET home_table=?2,home_id=?3,home_column=?4 WHERE id=?1",
   142	        params![id, to.0, to.1, to.2],
   143	    )?;
   144	    Ok(())
   145	}
   146	pub(super) fn transition(

   151	    report: &mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport,

   365	        if e.each { each::forward(tx,e,parent,child,&key,report)?; continue; }
   366	        super::activation::walk(tx, pt, |id, mut row| {
   367	            let saved:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT child_id FROM _extraction_rows WHERE definition=?1 AND parent_t=?2 AND parent_id=?3",params![key,pt,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   368	            let child_id = saved.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| id.to_owned());
   369	            let present = load(tx, ct, &child_id)?;
   370	            let independent:bool=if saved.is_none(){tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_rows WHERE child_t=?1 AND child_id=?2)",params![ct,child_id],|r|r.get(0))?}else{false};
   371	            if saved.is_none() && (present.is_some()||independent) {
   372	                return Err(refuse(format!(
   373	                    "extraction destination {}/{child_id} already has independent history",
   374	                    e.child
   375	                )));
   376	            }
   377	            if saved.is_some(){
   378	                let anchored:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND parent_t=?4 AND parent_id=?5 AND invalid=0)",params![ct,child_id,format!("$extract:{key}"),pt,id],|r|r.get(0))?;
   379	                if present.is_none()||!anchored{return Err(refuse("returning extraction lacks its receipt-owned child or valid parent-lifetime evidence; never recreate pruned/deleted history"))}
   380	            }
   381	            let mut image = present.unwrap_or_else(|| {
   382	                Value::Row(
   383	                    BTreeMap::from([
   384	                        ("id".into(), Value::Id(child_id.clone())),
   385	                        (e.reference.clone(), Value::Id(id.into())),
   386	                    ])
   387	                    .into(),
   388	                )
   389	            });
   390	            if image.field(&e.reference) != Ok(&Value::Id(id.into())) {
   391	                return Err(refuse("extraction cannot rebind a child parent"));
   392	            }
   393	            for (destination, source) in &e.fields {
   394	                let kind = &child.columns[destination];
   395	                let Some(v) = value(tx, pt, id, &row, source)? else {
   396	                    continue;
   397	                };
   398	                if asset(kind) {
   399	                    if image.field(destination).is_ok_and(|old| old != &v) {
   400	                        return Err(refuse(
   401	                            "extraction destination asset conflicts with its source",
   402	                        ));
   403	                    }
   404	                    retain_cell(tx, &key, pt, id, source, &v)?;
   405	                    transfer(
   406	                        tx,
   407	                        &v,
   408	                        (&e.parent, id, source),
   409	                        (&e.child, &child_id, destination),
   410	                    )?;
   411	                    fields(&mut image)?.insert(destination.clone(), v);
   412	                    fields(&mut row)?.remove(source);
   413	                    tx.conn().execute(
   414	                        "DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",
   415	                        params![pt, id, source],
   416	                    )?;
   417	                } else if saved.is_none() {
   418	                    fields(&mut image)?.insert(destination.clone(), v);
   419	                } else if image.field(destination).ok() != Some(&v) {
   420	                    return Err(refuse(
   421	                        "extraction immutable scalar copies disagree with the returning parent",
   422	                    ));
   423	                }
   424	            }
   425	            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, child);
   426	            write(tx, pt, id, &row)?;
   427	            write(tx, ct, &child_id, &image)?;
   428	            tx.conn().execute(
   429	                "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _extraction_rows VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",
   430	                params![key, pt, id, ct, child_id],
   431	            )?;
   432	            super::dormant::capture_refs(
   433	                tx,
   434	                ct,
   435	                &child_id,
   436	                &format!("$extract:{key}"),
   437	                &ColumnType::Ref {
   438	                    table: e.parent.clone(),
   439	                },
   440	                &Value::Id(id.into()),
   441	            )?;
   442	            count += 1;
   443	            Ok(())
   444	        })?;
   445	        report.backfills.push(format!(
   446	            "extract {}: prepared {count} child rows and moved existing asset homes",
   447	            e.child
   448	        ));
   449	    }
   450	    tx.set_meta("activation_population", "true")?;
   451	    Ok(())
   452	}
   453	pub(super) fn delete(tx: &Tx<'_>, t: i64, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   454	    if exists(tx)? {
   455	        each::delete(tx,t,id)?;
   456	        tx.conn().execute(
   457	            "DELETE FROM _extraction_cells WHERE parent_t=?1 AND parent_id=?2",
   458	            params![t, id],
   459	        )?;
   460	        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _extraction_rows WHERE (parent_t=?1 AND parent_id=?2) OR (child_t=?1 AND child_id=?2)",params![t,id])?;
   461	    }
   462	    Ok(())
   463	}
   464	
   465	pub(super) fn observe_write(
   466	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
   467	    t: i64,
   468	    id: &str,
   469	    row: &Value,
   470	    old: Option<&Value>,
   471	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   472	    if !exists(tx)? {
   473	        return Ok(());
   474	    }
   475	    let active:Vec<(String,String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT definition,parent_name,reference_column FROM _extraction_active WHERE child_t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
   476	    for (key, parent, column) in active {
   477	        let binding = format!("$extract:{key}");
   478	        let existing: bool = tx.conn().query_row(
   479	            "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3)",
   480	            params![t, id, binding],
   481	            |r| r.get(0),
   482	        )?;
   483	        if existing && old.is_some_and(|old| old.field(&column).ok() == row.field(&column).ok()) {
   484	            continue;
   485	        }
   486	        if let Ok(value) = row.field(&column) {
   487	            super::dormant::capture_refs(
   488	                tx,
   489	                t,
   490	                id,
   491	                &binding,
   492	                &ColumnType::Ref { table: parent },
   493	                value,
   494	            )?;
   495	        }
   496	    }
   497	    Ok(())
   498	}
   499	
   500	fn retain_cell(
   501	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
   502	    key: &str,
   503	    t: i64,
   504	    id: &str,
   505	    column: &str,
   506	    value: &Value,
   507	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   508	    let old: Option<String> = tx
   509	        .conn()
   510	        .query_row(
   511	            "SELECT data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",
   512	            params![t, id, column],
   513	            |r| r.get(0),
   514	        )
   515	        .optional()?;
   516	    if let Some(old) = old {
   517	        let old: Value = serde_json::from_str(&old).map_err(|e| refuse(e.to_string()))?;
   518	        if &old != value {
   519	            return Err(refuse(
   520	                "extraction has a distinct retained source cell; preserve both meanings in an authored bridge",
   521	            ));
   522	        }
   523	    }
   524	    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _extraction_cells VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5) ON CONFLICT(definition,parent_t,parent_id,column_name) DO UPDATE SET data=excluded.data",params![key,t,id,column,canonical_json(value)])?;
   525	    Ok(())
   526	}

    27	    match r.state {

    30	    }

   316	        .replacen(

   319	            1,

     1	//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
     2	use super::*;
     3	use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};
     4	
     5	pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
     6	    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
     7	}
     8	
     9	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
    10	pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    11	    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    12	    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    13	    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
    14	}
    15	
    16	/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
    17	pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    18	    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    19	    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    20	    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    21	    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    22	    loop {
    23	        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
    24	        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
    25	        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
    26	        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
    27	        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
    28	        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
    29	        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
    30	        let page_mark = meter.mark();
    31	        let mut limit = PAGE;
    32	        let mut page = loop {
    33	            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
    34	            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
    35	                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    36	            } else {
    37	                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    38	            };
    39	            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
    40	            {
    41	                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
    42	                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
    43	                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
    44	                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
    45	                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
    46	                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
    47	                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
    48	                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
    49	                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
    50	                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
    51	                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
    52	                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
    53	                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
    54	                    bytes = next;
    55	                    count += 1;
    56	                }
    57	            }
    58	            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
    59	            let query = if after.is_some() {
    60	                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    61	            } else {
    62	                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    63	            };
    64	            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
    65	            {
    66	                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
    67	                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
    68	                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
    69	            }
    70	            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
    71	            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
    72	            drop(page);
    73	            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
    74	        };
    75	        for (id, data) in &page {
    76	            let row_mark = meter.mark();
    77	            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
    78	            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
    79	            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
    80	            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
    81	            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
    82	            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
    83	            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
    84	            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
    85	        }
    86	        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
    87	        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
    88	        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
    89	        drop(page);
    90	        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    91	    }
    92	}
    93	
    94	#[cfg(test)]
    95	mod tests {
    96	    use super::*;

   106	            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
   107	        }
   108	        tx.commit().unwrap();
   109	        store
   110	    }

   113	    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
   114	        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
   115	        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   116	        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;

   120	                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
   121	            ]));
   122	            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
   123	            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
   124	        }
   125	        let mut calls = 0;
   126	        let mut first_heap = None;
   127	        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
   128	            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
   129	            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
   130	            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
   131	            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
   132	            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
   133	            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
   134	            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
   135	                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
   136	            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
   137	            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
   138	            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
   139	            calls += 1;
   140	            Ok(())

## 11. Wave-22 loop repairs (DX6)

Commission: Charlie Cheever's wave-22 fixes #5, #6 and #8 (2026-10-22).
Implementer: Codex in sb4-b22-dx6. This amends §10, the staging clock of
LLP 3000.000.000.002 §4, supplied sessions in LLP 3000.036.004 §15.4,
and the starter application of LLP 3000.013.002 §2. SEC7 owns security advice,
intent, campaigns, upload recipes and the password/roles recipe.
This proposal is subject to the requested fresh Muse and Codex reviews.

### Session input and compatibility

Seeded records preserve the actual signup session's kind alongside its
principal, token and numeric expiresAt. They can be passed directly as Session.
Online createClient accepts a legacy incomplete session literal as token-only
transport credentials: its token must be nonempty; partial principal, kind and
expiry metadata never establishes identity, refresh timing or authority. The
server resolves the bearer; session() is null and there is no refresh timer,
as for the existing token option. Complete shapes remain strictly validated
and privately snapshotted. Local/device session input remains complete-only.
E_SESSION lists every missing/invalid field without values and gives the exact
one-line online repair createClient({ url, token }). An ISO expiry in a complete
shape refuses instead of creating a NaN timer. Restored full sessions retain
the existing restore validation and server expiry semantics.

### Advances, maintenance and retained boundaries

ClockAdvance remains an object reporting now/fired/held; valueOf returns now
for arithmetic, ordering and new Date(result). This is JavaScript numeric
coercion, not strict-equality compatibility; typed callers use result.now.

For classified staging, advance(duration) preserves remaining ordinary session
lifetimes by default. The core advances valid full/read session created/expiry
fields, read caps and recoverable mint receipts by the same checked delta,
in the same transaction as existing worker-lease preservation, before publishing
the new process clock. Expired, capped, revoked or superseded authority cannot
become live. Selection uses the authority kernel and bounded pages; overflow
refuses atomically. Existing action delegated sessions and supervised service
families remain owned by the worker-lease path, without double shifting.
Historical admission timestamps are evidence, not an ongoing cap; application
deadlines/backoffs remain business time.

Preservation includes SDK custody, not just raw HTTP credentials. A staging
response for the exact request bearer can publish authoritative session expiry
and read cap beside the same locked business-time/revision snapshot, obtained
through a detached store-identity/liveness-checked authority read. No bearer,
new authority, mint, rotation or refresh is exposed. Production publishes no
such metadata. A failed detached timing read suppresses the clock sample,
including streaming-final samples, instead of publishing advanced business
time with stale credential metadata. A client reconciles only its current
bearer's matching request URL/header and monotonically increases its live
expiry/cap before clock listeners run. Local/device clients await durable
credential custody before publishing or persisting their business clock.
Online clients keep a volatile clock; their existing session listeners may
persist custody asynchronously. Authenticated restoration still validates
expired metadata with the staging probe; no online durability barrier is implied.
Heartbeat final frames carry the same matching-bearer timing or unavailable
evidence as ordinary response headers. Reconciliation and the corresponding
local/device clock publication are serialized: durable credential saves are awaited
before publishing or persisting the advanced clock. A failed save leaves the
clock unchanged, retains dirty credential timing for retry, and surfaces as a
transport failure; rotation/close fences also suppress stale clock publication.
App preparation retains its existing private credential staging: prepared local
clock offsets remain non-durable, including on discard/close, until the actual
App credential commit succeeds. Only then may clock persistence activate before
the scope is published. A later clock-save failure reports a signed-in warning
and retries through ordinary device work; it cannot roll back durable identity.
The existing per-invocation response cutoff (LLP 3000.013 §6 and §12.7) also
spans implicit scope retirement's local credential cleanup. A scope-owned
reference count retains capture before teardown and through each scheduled
local credential task, releasing on rollback or close without waiting for an
auth operation or remote revocation queue.
Capture occurs synchronously when the response decoder validates a body,
before caller Promise continuations can cross that cutoff.
An invocation retains a body validated while live through settlement; a
completed capture owner cannot reopen its cutoff through another begin or hold.
Retention preserves a decoded body, not a stale authentication classification.
An E_AUTH refusal remains scoped to the bearer revision actually dispatched:
rotation supersedes prior and intermediate retiring-bearer revisions, including
when the response was decoded before rotation or arrives after cancellation or
a retryable refresh failure. Recheck that authority when consuming retained
responses. A successor already dispatched during credential persistence remains
current; its own refusal may sign it out. Sign-out alone does not supersede a
same-bearer refusal, and sent outcomes and business refusals retain their ordinary
invocation custody. Retryable refresh failure still permits a same-id retry under
the current credential revision.
Rotated/logged-out credentials cannot be restored by a late response. Client
clocks are isolated, so one bearer's response cannot expire another before its
own timing is reconciled. Read startup verifies before scheduling expiry;
an expired restored metadata image requires a successful authenticated read-only
staging probe with matching viewer/scope and live timing. Production/offline
without this evidence keeps the existing expiry refusal. The local constructor
reconciles bootstrap timing before applying a restored device clock's expiry
check. Tests retain actual read clients and restore them across their old
absolute expiry, compare two independent bearers, and exercise explicit-expiry
and production controls. These headers are staging timing evidence only;
ordinary authority is still checked on every server admission. Real elapsed time still consumes
credentials. sessions:'expire' explicitly tests expiry with the existing
business clock. Dev retains its existing expiry behavior. Production rejects
all clock controls and never calls preservation. Restart keeps the persisted credential timestamps. Staging clock persistence
now follows §12; ordinary dev keeps its process-local offset.
This intentional staging-only simulated jump also shifts a read family's cap;
ordinary production read-cap immutability is unchanged.

advance(duration, {drain:false}) advances time and pauses background maintenance
before releasing the writer. This covers expiry sweeps, scheduled enqueue,
queued job chunks, asset collection, view recount and delivery maintenance,
so a mutation can exercise its own deadline check. Authorization and logical
row expiry still apply to ordinary queries/mutations. The pause lasts until
host.drain(), another draining advance, or process restart. drain() uses the
same authenticated endpoint with duration:'0ms', drain:true, advancing no time
and resuming normal coalesced maintenance. eachBoundary requires drain:true.
The HTTP report includes maintenancePaused; native CLI spells these controls
clock advance D --no-drain and clock drain. Failed/ambiguous requests are never
automatically replayed. A failed drain leaves its advanced time intact, its
report explicitly incomplete, and maintenance paused. Only a successful explicit
drain resumes it. drain() advances no time and safely continues existing queued
work/checkpoints; never repeat a lost advance to obtain that recovery.

A saved boundary is future-held only when it is strictly greater than the
boundary of the requested effective schedule window. Equality means that the
current window was already processed: report not-due, without consulting wall
time. Repeated advances inside that window and zero-time drains succeed without
allowHeld, including recovery of queued work after a failed drain completion.
Never enqueue the processed window again.
A draining advance whose saved future boundary blocks requested work refuses
E_CLOCK_HELD before clock, session or lease effects. The refusal names the
schedule, saved boundary, next unprocessed window and recovery duration to
reach the saved boundary from the unchanged process clock, not from the rejected
requested time. Relative recovery hints in accepted reports likewise use the
actual process clock, including when a disclosure floor is ahead of it.
A held report sets heldUntil to that saved boundary and
nextBoundary to the next unprocessed boundary. Catching up to heldUntil
reports not-due and never re-enqueues the processed window; reaching nextBoundary
fires once. Fresh staging storage and continuing with the original offset are
recovery choices; saved markers never rewind. allowHeld:true (CLI --allow-held) explicitly accepts a
retained boundary. Intentional drain:false pauses and dev --hold-schedules are
explicit holds. not-due/initialization remains a successful reported outcome.
Control authorization/classification is checked again under the actual writer.

### Upgrade advice and future formats
## 12. Wave-24 loop repairs (DX7)

Commission: Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; implementer: Codex in sb4-b24-dx7.
This amends §11, LLP 3000.036 §22.8 and the ordinary rule-helper exclusion.
SEC8 owns security questionnaire/dispositions, presence intent, invariant test
coverage and lifecycle advice; DX7 only reports their existing verdicts.
Implementation and tests accompany this amendment; review readiness is distinct
from author acceptance.

### Durable staging time

A staging store owns its cumulative offset and latest explicitly checkpointed
business time. The additive typed metadata record also retains a pending
maintenance pause. Production ignores this record and rejects clock controls;
ordinary dev retains its process-local clock/reset contract. No release-format
change or filesystem sidecar is needed. Store adapters use their existing
metadata transaction and fencing, including Postgres.

Startup resumes at the maximum of wall plus saved offset, checkpointed time,
and the persisted time_audience_clock disclosure floor (LLP 3000.000.000.002
§4), plus the maximum retained commit stamp and schedule boundary even when a
checkpoint exists. A fresh store with no evidence starts at wall time. Unknown
metadata is preserved by ordinary old-binary adoptions; if an older binary or
manual restore removes it, recover time from these retained floors, with no
claim to recover a deleted pause. Explicit startup
offsets (serve --clock-offset and startServe({clockOffset})) are absolute
offsets from wall time, clamped to the resumed floor;
restarting never adds the same offset twice or rewinds saved boundaries.
The resulting effective offset is persisted at startup, preserving the saved
paused flag exactly. This is the maximum of five named evidence values: wall
plus offset, checkpointed now, time_audience_clock, recent commit maximum and
latest schedule boundary (plus an explicitly requested offset floor). Removing
all time evidence by restoring/recreating a store can return it to wall time;
this is the intentional independent-test reset path, never an in-place rewind.
Old binaries do not enforce the pause even when they preserve its record;
downgrade is not a way to run a paused proof under these guarantees. Malformed checkpoints
refuse startup rather than silently resetting. Downgrades to older binaries
ignore this additive record and retain their historical process-local behavior.

Each accepted staging advance checkpoints offset, time and paused=true in the
same transaction that preserves eligible sessions and worker leases, before
publishing the process clock or doing maintenance. E_CLOCK_HELD and input validation precede this transaction. A definite
storage abort rolls back all those effects. Postgres executes the existing
fenced writer transaction containing offset/time/pause and session/lease edits.
An uncertain COMMIT may already have committed: E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN fences
the host from further admission/maintenance, then shuts it down. Restart reads
the durable checkpoint before serving; never replay a credential shift to
reconcile it. An uncertain pause-clear may already have cleared it, so only
definite failure guarantees retention of the durable pause. Fault injection
covers both write boundaries. A lost reply is still ambiguous and never automatically
replayed. drain:false, a failed drain and an interrupted drain retain the pause
across restart. A successful drain durably clears it only after due work has
finished. If that final write fails, report an incomplete drain and retain the
pause. Startup writes a checkpoint with zero delta and never preserves credentials.
Each advance computes its delta from the resumed effective clock and writes
credential timestamps and checkpoint in one commit: the checkpoint itself is
the durable completion evidence; there is no separate applied marker. A restart
does not shift already-preserved credentials again. Clock
status/advance reports say the offset belongs to this staging store. Coalescing,
eachBoundary, current-window equality and explicit allowHeld keep §11 semantics.

   493	                }
   494	            }

   505	        }

   507	            .execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1", [t])?;

   510	    }
   511	    let mut authority_sources=previous.map(|s|s.tables.clone()).unwrap_or_default();

   513	    crate::engine::through_activation::reconcile_manual(tx,next,&authority_sources,now)?;

   522	        }

   526	        } else {0};

   529	            Ok(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero().fixed(if fetch {parent_scratch} else {0}))
   530	        };
   531	        super::activation_walk::walk_metered_checked(tx,t,crate::rows::adoption::Work::DECODE,if parent_scratch>0 {Some(&scratch)} else {None},|id,mut row,meter| {

   538	                if restoring.iter().any(|(n,_)|n==name) {super::authority_origin::record(tx,t,id,old)?;}
   539	            }

   541	            super::authority_origin::overlay(tx,t,id,old,table,&mut row,values)?;
   542	            super::backfills::preserve_native_absence(&mut row,&native);
   543	            tx.conn().execute(
   544	                "UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",
   545	                params![canonical_json(&row), t, id],
   546	            )?;
   547	            Ok(())
   548	        })?;
   549	    }
   550	    for name in next.tables.keys() {
   551	        if next.tables[name].through.is_some() && tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1)",[tx.num(name)?],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {retained_through.insert(name.clone());}
   552	    }

   146	fn int_range(kind: &ColumnType) -> bool {
   147	    match kind {
   148	        ColumnType::IntRange { .. } => true,
   149	        ColumnType::Optional(inner) | ColumnType::List { of: inner, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of: inner, .. } => int_range(inner),
   150	        _ => false,
   151	    }
   152	}
   153	
   154	fn ordered_units(table: &Table, rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   155	    let units = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(f) if matches!(table.columns.get(f), Some(ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. })));
   156	    match rule {
   157	        RuleExpr::Compare(_, l, r) => units(l) || units(r),
   158	        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|item| ordered_units(table, item)),
   159	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => ordered_units(table, inner),
   160	        _ => false,

     1	//! Table names may change; numeric row, reference and receipt lifetimes do not.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — renames are identity moves, never row copies or merges.
     3	use super::*;
     4	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     5	pub(super) const VERSION:&str="snapback4-release-store-10";
     6	fn refuse(s:impl Into<String>)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Structural{code:"E_ADOPT",message:s.into()}}
     7	
     8	// Only structural table identifiers change. Literal values, column names,
     9	// operation names and authored defaults are never table identifiers.
    10	fn identifiers(value:&mut serde_json::Value,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>) {
    11	    match value {
    12	        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for x in xs {identifiers(x,map)},
    13	        serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>for (key,x) in fields {
    14	            if key=="Literal" || key=="defaults" {continue;}
    15	            if matches!(key.as_str(),"table"|"parent") {if let Some(name)=x.as_str().and_then(|s|map.get(s)){*x=serde_json::Value::String(name.clone());continue;}}
    16	            identifiers(x,map);
    17	        },_=>()
    18	    }
    19	}
    20	fn mapped(name:&str,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->String {map.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||name.into())}
    21	fn remap<T:serde::Serialize+serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(value:&T,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<T,RuntimeError> {
    22	    let mut json=serde_json::to_value(value).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,map);
    23	    serde_json::from_value(json).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))
    24	}
    25	pub(crate) fn schema(mut schema:Schema,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<Schema,RuntimeError> {
    26	    if map.is_empty(){return Ok(schema)}
    27	    schema.tables=std::mem::take(&mut schema.tables).into_iter().map(|(name,table)|Ok((mapped(&name,map),remap(&table,map)?))).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    28	    for m in &mut schema.maintains {*m=remap(m,map)?;m.source=mapped(&m.source,map);m.target=mapped(&m.target,map);}
    29	    for f in &mut schema.backfills {fill(f,map,false)?;}
    30	    for f in &mut schema.downfills {fill(f,map,true)?;}
    31	    for e in &mut schema.extractions {e.child=mapped(&e.child,map);}
    32	    // Remaining table-bearing structural metadata is normalized through the
    33	    // same typed codec; maintains' source/target are handled explicitly above.
    34	    // These maps contain column identifiers, including arbitrary keys such as
    35	    // "parent". Normalize their typed table slots, never their column strings.
    36	    let mut extractions=std::mem::take(&mut schema.extractions);
    37	    for e in &mut extractions {e.parent=mapped(&e.parent,map);}
    38	    let columns=std::mem::take(&mut schema.asset_renames).into_iter().map(|(name,columns)|(mapped(&name,map),columns)).collect();
    39	    schema.table_renames.clear();schema=remap(&schema,map)?;
    40	    schema.extractions=extractions;schema.asset_renames=columns;
    41	    Ok(schema)
    42	}
    43	fn fill(f:&mut crate::schema::backfill::Backfill,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>,down:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    44	    *f=remap(f,map)?;
    45	    f.program.name=format!("{}{}{}",if down{"down"}else{"backfill"},f.table,f.name);Ok(())
    46	}
    47	pub(super) fn prepare(conn:&Connection,next:&Schema,catalog:&mut BTreeMap<String,i64>)->Result<BTreeMap<String,String>,RuntimeError> {
    48	    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_table_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    49	    if !exists && next.table_renames.is_empty(){return Ok(BTreeMap::new())}
    50	    conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _table_renames(name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,origin TEXT NOT NULL) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    51	    let mut names:BTreeMap<String,String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name,origin FROM _table_renames")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    52	    for (new,old) in &next.table_renames {
    53	        if new==old || !next.tables.contains_key(new) || next.tables.contains_key(old){return Err(refuse("table rename requires a distinct predecessor-only source"))}
    54	        let origin=names.get(old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||old.clone());
    55	        if names.get(new).is_some_and(|root|root!=&origin){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with an independent retained identity")))}
    56	        if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(catalog.get(old),catalog.get(new)) {if a!=b{return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} cannot merge existing table lifetimes")))}}
    57	        for name in [old,new] {names.insert(name.clone(),origin.clone());conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _table_renames VALUES(?1,?2)",params![name,origin])?;}
    58	    }
    59	    let mut selected=BTreeMap::<String,String>::new();
    60	    for name in next.tables.keys() {if let Some(root)=names.get(name) {if let Some(other)=selected.insert(root.clone(),name.clone()){return Err(refuse(format!("table aliases {other} and {name} cannot be active together")))}}}
    61	    let map:BTreeMap<String,String>=names.iter().filter_map(|(name,root)|selected.get(root).filter(|new|*new!=name).map(|new|(name.clone(),new.clone()))).collect();
    62	    for (old,new) in &map {
    63	        if !catalog.contains_key(old){continue;}
    64	        if catalog.contains_key(new){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with retained table storage")))}
    65	        let entries:Vec<_>=catalog.iter().filter(|(key,_)|*key==old || key.starts_with(&format!("{old}."))).map(|(k,v)|(k.clone(),*v)).collect();
    66	        for (key,num) in entries {let renamed=format!("{new}{}",&key[old.len()..]);conn.execute("UPDATE _catalog SET name=?1 WHERE name=?2",params![renamed,key])?;catalog.remove(&key);catalog.insert(renamed,num);}
    67	        conn.execute("UPDATE _assets SET home_table=?1 WHERE home_table=?2",params![new,old])?;
    68	        for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_indexes"] {
    69	            if conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET table_name=?1 WHERE table_name=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
    70	        }
    71	    }
    72	    // Retained typed definitions must use the current public table names too.
    73	    for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_narrow_rows"] {
    74	        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
    75	        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT definition,definition FROM {table} GROUP BY definition"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    76	        for (old,text) in rows {let mut json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,&map);let new=canonical_json(&json);if old!=new {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}}
    77	    }
    78	    history(conn,&map)?;
    79	    conn.execute("UPDATE _meta SET value=?1 WHERE key='version'",[VERSION])?;
    80	    Ok(map)
    81	}
    82	fn history(conn:&Connection,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    83	    for (table,down) in [("_backfill_definitions",false),("_downfill_definitions",true)] {
    84	        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
    85	        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT digest,definition FROM {table}"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    86	        for (old,text) in rows {let mut f:crate::schema::backfill::Backfill=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;fill(&mut f,map,down)?;let new=if down {f.down_key()}else{f.key()};if old==new{continue;}
    87	            conn.execute(&format!("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO {table} VALUES(?1,?2)"),params![new,canonical_json(&f)])?;
    88	            for ledger in ["_backfill_evidence","_backfill_pruned","_backfill_absence"] {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {ledger} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
    89	            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$native:{new}"),format!("$native:{old}")])?;
    90	            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$authored:{new}"),format!("$authored:{old}")])?;
    91	            for c in f.inputs.keys() {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_refs SET column_name=?1 WHERE column_name=?2",params![format!("$backfill:{new}:{c}"),format!("$backfill:{old}:{c}")])?;}
    92	            conn.execute(&format!("DELETE FROM {table} WHERE digest=?1"),[old])?;
    93	        }
    94	    }Ok(())
    95	}

    41	export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
    42	  const value = ms ?? fallback;
    43	  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
    44	  return value;
    45	}
    46	/** @throws deadline expires; work rejection propagates. */
    47	export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
    48	  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
    49	  try {
    50	    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
    51	      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    52	    })]);
    53	  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
    54	}
    55	/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
    56	/** @throws AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. */
    57	export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
    58	  const errors: unknown[] = [];
    59	  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    60	    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    61	    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
    62	  }
    63	  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
    64	}
    65	/** @throws invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. */
    66	export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {
    67	  deadline(graceMs, 5000);
    68	  if (child && "url" in child && typeof child.url === "string" && typeof child.stop === "function") {
    69	    await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => child.stop()), graceMs + 10_000, "host stop"); return;
    70	  }
    71	  if (!child || !("kill" in child) || typeof child.kill !== "function" || typeof child.once !== "function"
    72	      || !(child.exitCode === null || typeof child.exitCode === "number")
    73	      || !(child.signalCode === null || typeof child.signalCode === "string")) {
    74	    throw new TypeError("stopChild needs a ChildProcess or { url, stop }");
    75	  }
    76	  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) { await groupGone(child); return; }
    77	  let done!: () => void;
    78	  const exited = new Promise<void>(resolve => { done = resolve; });
    79	  child.once("exit", done);
    80	  child.once("error", done);
    81	  try {
    82	    child.kill("SIGTERM");
    83	    try { await bounded(exited, graceMs, "SIGTERM"); }
    84	    catch {
    85	      child.kill("SIGKILL");
    86	      await bounded(exited, 5000, "SIGKILL reap");
    87	    }
    88	  } finally { child.removeListener("exit", done); child.removeListener("error", done); }
    89	  await groupGone(child);
    90	}
    91	async function groupGone(child: ChildProcess): Promise<void> {
    92	  const pid = privateGroups.get(child);
    93	  if (!pid || process.platform === "win32") return;
    94	  const existing = groupClosures.get(child);
    95	  if (existing) return existing;
    96	  const cleanup = (async () => {
    97	    const expires = performance.now() + 5000;
    98	    for (;;) {
    99	      try { process.kill(-pid, 0); }
   100	      catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ESRCH") return; throw error; }
   101	      const remaining = expires - performance.now();
   102	      if (remaining <= 0) throw Error("proof process-group cleanup timed out after 5000 ms");
   103	      await sleep(Math.min(10, remaining));
   104	    }
   105	  })();
   106	  groupClosures.set(child, cleanup);
   107	  return cleanup;
   108	}
   109	/** @throws partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. */
   110	export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
   111	  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
   112	  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
   113	  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)
   114	      || Number(low) < 1 || Number(high) > 65535 || Number(low) > Number(high)) {
   115	    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
   116	  }
   117	  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;
   118	}
   119	/** App-local evidence survives harness cleanup of its temporary root. */
   120	/** @throws None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. */
   121	export function logsDir(options: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> = {}): string {
   122	  return resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", options.logsDir ?? "logs");
   123	}
   124	export function logged(command: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}) {
   125	  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), id = randomUUID();
   126	  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
   127	  const logs = { stdout: join(directory, `${id}.stdout.log`), stderr: join(directory, `${id}.stderr.log`) };
   128	  if (options.secret) writeFileSync(logs.stdout, "stdout withheld: secret command (returned only to caller)\n", { flag: "wx", mode: 0o600 });
   129	  const out = options.secret ? undefined : openSync(logs.stdout, "wx", 0o600), err = openSync(logs.stderr, "wx", 0o600);
   130	  const grouped = options.processGroup === true && process.platform !== "win32";
   131	  const executable = grouped ? process.execPath : command;
   132	  const executableArgs = grouped ? [resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-command.js", import.meta.url))), command, ...args] : [...args];
   133	  let child: ChildProcess;
   134	  try { child = spawn(executable, executableArgs, { cwd, detached: grouped || (options.detached ?? false), env: { ...(options.env ?? process.env), SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LAUNCH: id }, stdio: [grouped || options.livenessPipe ? "pipe" : "ignore", options.secret ? "pipe" : out!, err, ...(grouped ? ["ipc" as const] : [])] }); }
   135	  finally { if (out !== undefined) closeSync(out); closeSync(err); }
   136	  if (grouped && child.pid) privateGroups.set(child, child.pid);
   137	  // Secret stdout never reaches a file, even briefly. Callers still receive it.
   138	  let stdout = "";
   139	  child.stdout?.setEncoding("utf8");
   140	  child.stdout?.on("data", chunk => { stdout += chunk; });
   141	  register(child);
   142	  child.stdin?.on("error", () => {});
   143	  let error: Error | undefined;
   144	  let actualPid = child.pid;
   145	  let actualCode: number | null = null;
   146	  let actualSignal: NodeJS.Signals | null = null;
   147	  let startedResolve!: () => void, startedReject!: (error: Error) => void, startedSettled = false;
   148	  const started = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { startedResolve = resolve; startedReject = reject; });
   149	  void started.catch(() => {}); // command() observes close/error instead; host startup awaits this promise.
   150	  const didStart = () => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedResolve(); } };
   151	  const failedStart = (value: unknown) => { if (!startedSettled) { startedSettled = true; startedReject(value instanceof Error ? value : Error(String(value))); } };
   152	  if (!grouped) didStart();
   153	  child.on("message", message => {
   154	    const event = message as {type?:string;pid?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:NodeJS.Signals|null;error?:string};
   155	    if (event.type === "started" && typeof event.pid === "number") { actualPid = event.pid; didStart(); }
   156	    if (event.type === "result") { actualCode = event.code ?? null; actualSignal = event.signal ?? null; if (event.error) error = Error(event.error); }
   157	  });
   158	  child.on("error", value => { error = value; failedStart(value); });
   159	  const closed = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => child.once("close", () => {
   160	    failedStart(error ?? Error("proof command exited before its process-group handshake"));
   161	    groupGone(child).then(resolve,reject);
   162	  }));
   163	  return { child, logs, id, closed, started, pid: () => actualPid, code: () => grouped ? actualCode : child.exitCode, signal: () => grouped ? actualSignal : child.signalCode, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
   164	}
   165	export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
   166	export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
   167	  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
   168	  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
   169	  while (performance.now() < end) {
   170	    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
   171	    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
   172	      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
   173	    }
   174	    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
   175	      const text = logText(path);
   176	      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
   177	      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
   178	        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
   179	        if (url) return url;
   180	      }
   181	      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
   182	    }
   183	    await sleep(25);
   184	  }
   185	  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
   186	}
